# #2 v2 — Distributional Learning of Multiple Context-Free Languages under Fixed Finite-Semigroup Typing: Local Groups and the Length of Characteristic Data

**Author:** Takayuki Kuriyama
**Target journal:** Theoretical Computer Science (to be submitted after the decision on the companion TCS-D-26-00494)
**Status:** **A＋A+ revision (2026-11-08, (211), merged into main (212))**: Theorem A and its simultaneous-shortening technique at the centre, with the restored non-context-free targets showing that local groups and noncommutative groups are both needed; Theorem C kept as the fan-out-one benchmark; English 46 pages, Japanese 52 pages. Before: focused version (42 pages after (161); 41 at (157), 2026-11-06): one claim — local groups are the boundary of polynomial characteristic data; all removed material is in [`parked/2026-11-06_focus/`](parked/2026-11-06_focus/). Earlier external reviews (ChatGPT comparison, DeepSeek referee, two ChatGPT TCS-style referee reports) and the citation audit were addressed; an independent reading of the focused version is pending; Japanese version pending.

This folder is the restructured ("new-born") version of paper #2. The mathematics is taken from the frozen v1 in [`archive-v1/`](archive-v1/) and is organized around one claim and three main theorems:

- **Theorem A** — local groups: characteristic data $O(\|G\|^2\bar\theta_G)$ at every fan-out.
- §5.4 — both features of Theorem A are needed on non-context-free targets of fan-out two: `prop:lg-needed-target` (a local-group typing, no window--group typing) and `prop:nonabelian-needed-target` (a window--$S_3$ typing, no window--abelian typing); proofs in the appendix `app:separating-targets`.
- **Theorem B** — the linear factor: $\Omega(\|G\|\bar\theta_G)$ under every typing at every fan-out (alphabet condition); $\Theta(\|G\|\bar\theta_G)$ for the trivial typing.
- **Theorem C** — the boundary at fan-out one: pseudovarieties with uniform polynomial data ⇔ contained in $\mathbf{LG}$; outside $\mathbf{LG}$ explicit targets need exponential data.
- Section 8 — the exponential family does not lift to fan-out two (`thm:doubling-no-lift`). Whether $\mathbf{LG}$ remains the boundary at fan-out two is Open Problem `op:fanout-two`.

**Start here:** [`INSTRUCTIONS_FOR_AI.md`](INSTRUCTIONS_FOR_AI.md) (status, structure, next phases, rules, checklist). Latest handoff: [`HANDOFF_2026-11-02.md`](HANDOFF_2026-11-02.md) (previous: `HANDOFF_2026-11-01.md`).

**Research on `op:fanout-two` (2026-11-01):** see [`reviews/STATUS_sl_fanout2_2026-11-01.md`](reviews/STATUS_sl_fanout2_2026-11-01.md). The two-letter content typing has uniform polynomial data at every fan-out $f\ge2$: `thm:two-letter-content` (§8, proof in Appendix H, added 2026-11-01, extended to all $f\ge2$ on 2026-11-02); fan-out one and three letters open. **2026-11-07:** for languages cut out by one-letter-one-edge paths and abelian cosets per content, $\mu_{\mathtt{ab}}\le\max\{9(r+1)v\theta,226v^3\theta^3\}$ in every presentation (Theorem H, (179)(180)); the general antichain width remains open.

## Files

- `main.tex` — English master (64 pages, `prop:zeta-capacity` added 2026-11-09 and generalized to content typings, README (241), (244); before: 63 pages, compressed 2026-11-09, README (236); before: 65 pages, Appendix H rewritten for isolated letters 2026-11-09, README (235); before: 62 pages, parked two-letter and U₁ results restored 2026-11-09, README (229); before: 52 pages, two-letter theorem at fan-out one added 2026-11-09, README (228); before: 46 pages, A＋A+ revision of 2026-11-08, README (211); before: 42 pages, focused version of 2026-11-06, README (157), (159), (161)). Japanese: `japanese/main_JP.tex` (73 pages, LuaLaTeX). Builds with `latexmk -pdf main.tex`, no warnings; `python3 tools/check_port.py` passes.
- `parked/2026-11-06_focus/` — everything removed by the focused version: the whole 59-page manuscript before the cut (`main_before_focus_59pp.tex`), the removed blocks (`removed_blocks.tex`, read by `check_port.py`) and an index (`README.md`).
- `parked/2026-11-06_intro/` — the English and Japanese introductions before the rewrite of README (174).
- `parked/2026-11-06_abstract/` — the English and Japanese abstracts before the rewrite of README (172).
- `parked/parked_from_v1.tex` — v1 material not used in v2 (one-letter cyclic typing).
- `parked/parked_fanout2_and_comparison.tex` — material removed by the core-only version (Theorem C(i) on the binary typing at fan-out two with its proof, the comparison appendix and $L_R$ example, the self-contained substitutability proof of the bracketed family, full proofs of polynomial time and Gold identification).
- `parked/parked_u1_typing_level.tex` — material removed by the 2026-10-31 shortening (typing-level results on $U_1$, constant weight, three-letter linear factor, target separations, rank one); seed of a separate paper on S1/S2.
- `archive-v1/` — the closed v1 (former `02_fixed-h-mcfg/`, arXiv:2605.11644): English and Japanese sources, its README with the full v1 change log, and `revision/` (all research reports and check scripts up to 2026-10-31). Frozen.
- `reviews/` — external reviews and audits of v2, and the 2026-11-01 research reports and prompts on fan-out two (summary: `STATUS_sl_fanout2_2026-11-01.md`): ChatGPT comparison v1/v2 (2026-10-30), DeepSeek referee report, ChatGPT re-reading of two proofs, citation audit (all 2026-10-31).
- `tools/check_singleton.py` — recheck of the former Appendix `app:singleton` (now parked) (Table `tab:singleton-certificates` and `lem:app-singleton-runs`, lengths 7--14); the proofs do not depend on it.
- `tools/check_port.py` — checks that every v1 label is in v2 or parked, and lists ported statements that differ from v1.
- `INSTRUCTIONS_FOR_AI.md`, `PAPER.yaml`.

- **2026-11-09 (240)** Report on the prompt (239), **written by another Claude session**: `reviews/claude_fanout2_U1_kzeta_2026-11-09.md` + `reviews/check_claude_fanout2_U1_kzeta_2026-11-09.py`. Checked by this session (every step of Theorem 1, Proposition 2, C(c), Lemma 5, Corollary 6 read; script rerun with `python3 -I`, 5 s, output as reported). **Theorem 1 [correct]:** for any reduced $\varepsilon$-free nondeleting MCFG (any fan-out and rank, any $U_1$ typing, **no substitutability**), with $\kappa=\sup_{w\in L}\#_\zeta(w)<\infty$: $\mu_1\le\max\{\bar\theta_G,\kappa|V|(\rho_{\max}+r\bar\theta_G)\}\le2\kappa\|G\|^2\bar\theta_G$ (Knuth argmin tree with distinct labels on paths; heavy labels $\lambda>\bar\theta$ have capacity $\ge1$; capacity superadditive, so at most $\kappa$ minimal heavy nodes). So Problem K is positive, and the remark of (239) that substitutability is essential for $k\ge2$ was wrong. **Proposition 2 [correct]:** $B_n$ ($\|G\|=12n+7$, $\theta=3$, $\mu_1=2^n$, $\kappa=2^{n-1}$) satisfies variant K′ with $k=2$, so K′ fails without substitutability; $L(B_n)\notin\mathcal C_{1,s_U}$. Its use as evidence that the $k$-dependence of Theorem 1 cannot be removed concerns grammars outside the class only. **C(c), Lemma 5, Corollary 6 [correct]:** in $\mathcal C_{f,s}$ two words of $L$ of equal type have equal 1-distributions; for a $U_1$ typing, $w\in L$ of type $\zeta$ and $uwv\in L$ give $u^nwv^n\in L$, hence $uv\in\Sigma_1^*$ if $\kappa<\infty$. **Correction:** N6's sentence "no counterexample with $\kappa<\infty$" is false as written — N5 only requires $\kappa(L_n)$ superpolynomial along the family; each $L_n$ may be finite (as for the fan-out-one CE family, $\kappa=2^n$). Minor: the $B_2$ violation printed by the script is $(\mathtt c)$ vs $(\mathtt{ca})$, context $\square\mathtt{aa}$, giving $\mathtt{caaa}\notin L$. **Consequence for `op:fanout-two`:** a superpolynomial rare-type-1 lower bound needs $\kappa(L_n)\ge\mu_1/(|V|(\rho_{\max}+r\bar\theta))$, superpolynomial in $\|G\|,\bar\theta$. Not entered in the manuscript. Open next: transfer to $\mu_{\mathtt{ab}}$ for content typings (missing letters do not consume capacity), negative designs compatible with Corollary 6.

Results: (1) Lemma PC and $P(\omega_S)=\{(0,0)\}$ correct; Proposition DD correct after one repair — the diagonality of all $P_j$ needs Theorem 1/Corollary 2 of (208), not only (E1)(E2); the placement equations of a doubling chain have exactly two solutions (all even, or all positions), and if the lowest $\mathtt b$-level is $E_k$ any divisor of $2^k$ can be the step. (2) **Theorem D:** for finite $L\in\mathcal C_{1,s_{U,2}}$, if $G$ has rules $E_j\to E_{j-1}E_{j-1}$ ($k<j\le n$) with $E_k$ heavy (pure yield $\mathtt a^c$, $c>\bar\theta$), then $\|G\|+2\ge 3^{M/3-1}/((M+1)((M+1)\bar\theta+c+1)^2(M\bar\theta+1)^2)$, $M=n-k$; so heavy doubling chains have $O(\log(\|G\|\bar\theta))$ levels and, for the doubling chains of the prompt, $N\le\mathrm{poly}(\|G\|,\bar\theta)$. Proof: the yields collapse to one base ($\omega_k\mathtt a^c\equiv\mathtt a^c\omega_k\equiv\omega_k\omega_k$, $\omega_j\equiv\mathtt a^{(2^{j-k}-1)c}\omega_k$), membership of code sequences is decided by their value (antichain lemma), and an abstract counting Theorem C (weighted centroid node, records $(X,p,q,\text{crossings})$, distinct subset sums) gives the bound. (3) General $T$: Proposition S (the step is inherited downwards from any node with two placements; some label has step $\le4(r+1)\bar\theta|V|^2$); the remaining core is **Problem C** — extracting $\omega(\log(\|G\|\bar\theta r))$ codes that collapse to one base when $T$ has no twin nodes (Fibonacci-type additive chains). (4) No counterexample; Fibonacci seeds give antichain violations in 90/90 partial closures. op:S1 not settled.

Conjecture: the class of $\omega_n$ contains all code sequences of value $N$ (any order, parity conditions), so a Theorem-M-type count forces grammar size $2^{\Omega(n)}$; the general target of route P is the dichotomy "antichain violation, or exponentially many values among forced classes". Theorem 4 of (208) is the one-core case. A counterexample needs a weighted code of short cores with exponentially growing values and consistent decoding — the two-letter form of (106)(b) and of the bounded-cores result (122). - **2026-11-08 (206)** **Astra's report on cancellative-monoid preimages** (prompt (205)): `reviews/astra_cancellative_preimage_2026-11-08.md`. No counterexample; negative results in restricted ranges. Checked by Claude (proofs read; the appendix script rerun, output identical: 30 words, 435 relations, 113 Adian single relations, 94,395 pairs, 66,559 of full Parikh rank, 0 of them Adian-acyclic; complete closures for $K\le64$ substitutable). **Theorem A (Astra):** on two letters, if every word of $L(G)$ satisfies $\alpha|w|_{\mathtt a}+\beta|w|_{\mathtt b}=\alpha N$ with $(\alpha,\beta)\ne(0,0)$ of any sign, the shortest pure $\mathtt a$-word has length $\le(r+1)\bar\theta_G^2$ (lowest heavy node bounds the normalized weight of $\mathtt b$ by $(r+1)\bar\theta_G$) — improves (100) from cubic/nonnegative to quadratic/any sign on two letters and proves (O4) of (204) independently. Prop. B: two-letter one-relator monoids always map nontrivially to $\mathbb Q$; Prop. C: two-letter Adian-acyclic presentations have at most one relation. Prop. D: if the image monoid is a group and one preimage is context-free, the group is virtually free (Muller–Schupp/Dunwoody), so infinite-order elements are undistorted — only proper submonoids remain. Examples: $C_2*C_3$ ((C1) with CF preimages, no distortion); $T_K=\langle\mathtt a,\mathtt b\mid\mathtt a^K=\mathtt b^2\rangle$ (finite preimage $\{\mathtt a^K,\mathtt b^2\}$; every CFG has thickness $\ge K$). Correction to (204): condition (C2) as stated ("poly(log n) positive words") is stronger than necessary; only short ($\le\bar\theta$) positive representatives of the target and heavy-node powers are needed; (C1) concerns the image monoid. Sprint STATUS updated. Manuscript unchanged.

- **2026-11-08 (204)** Sprint `op:S1`, route N (Claude; observation with proof, not independently checked; sprint STATUS item (8)): **cancellative-monoid preimages.** For a cancellative monoid $M$ and $\psi:\{\mathtt a,\mathtt b\}^*\to M$, $L=\psi^{-1}(m)$ is Clark–Eyraud substitutable; if $\mathtt a^N\in L$, then $L\setminus\{\mathtt a^N\}\in\Cmcf{1}{c}$ and characteristic samples for $c$ and $s_{U,2}$ must contain $\mathtt a^N$ (consistent with (N1)). Unlike groups, $L$ may be finite (positive Baumslag–Solitar monoid). Necessary condition: the group generated by $\psi(\mathtt a),\psi(\mathtt b)$ has finite abelianization — for a homomorphism $\lambda$ to $\mathbb Q$, the short yields of heavy nodes give $\lambda(\omega_B)=n_B\lambda(\mathtt a)$; $\lambda(\mathtt a)>0$ forces a nonnegative weight level and the bound of (100), $\lambda(\mathtt a)=0$ forces $\lambda(\mathtt b)=0$. So BS(1,2) is excluded, finite groups by Theorem G; a counterexample of this kind needs an infinite group with finite abelianization, exponential distortion of $\psi(\mathtt a)$ inside the positive monoid, and a small thin CFG for the positive preimage (e.g. property-(T) lattices — both properties unknown there). Manuscript unchanged.

- **2026-11-08 (201)** **Problem G answered positively** (Claude; not independently checked): `reviews/claude_problemG_mixing_2026-11-08.md` §8. **Lemma R1′:** with $\sigma=\alpha^{-1}\beta$ and $s_j=\alpha^{-j}\sigma\alpha^j$, $\varphi(w)=\alpha^{|w|}s_{|w|-1}^{\varepsilon_1}\cdots s_0^{\varepsilon_{|w|}}$, the $s_j$ generate $H_0$; $\alpha^d$ is a product of $\lambda\le(1+\log_2B)^2B^2$ factors $s_j^{\pm1}$ ($B=(r+2)\bar\theta_G$; Bezout combination of $u_S$ and the rule relators $e_Ru_X^{-1}$); so $H_0$ has diameter $D'\le\gamma(1+\lambda)$ in every window of $w=2\max\{\gamma,\lambda\}+1$ consecutive $s_j$ ($\gamma$ the symmetric diameter), each window of lazy steps contracts every nontrivial irreducible representation by $1-1/(32wD'^2)$, and the sup-mixing time is $t\le w+32w^2D'^2\ln(2|H|)$. **Theorem G:** $\operatorname{ord}\varphi(\mathtt a)\le|H|\le C(r\|G\|\bar\theta_G)^\kappa$ for absolute (ineffective) $C,\kappa$: if $\gamma<(|H|/5)^{1/20}$, Theorem M and Lemma R1′; otherwise Breuillard–Tointon Theorem 1.3 gives a quotient with a bounded-index abelian subgroup, Lemma R3 bounds it and hence $\gamma$, then Theorem M. Consequence: the group-preimage route to a negative answer of `op:S1` (fan-out separation, Corollary A1 of (51)) is closed; `op:S1` itself stays open. Items to check independently are listed in §8.7. Manuscript unchanged.

- **2026-11-08 (199)** Observation (N2) of (198) written out as a proof (Claude; not independently checked): `reviews/claude_problemG_mixing_2026-11-08.md`. **Theorem M:** for a finite group $H$, a monoid morphism $\varphi:\{\mathtt a,\mathtt b\}^*\to H$ onto $H$ and any $\varepsilon$-free CFG $G$ of $\varphi^{-1}(1)\cap\Sigma^+$, $|H|\le\frac92\theta_S(\|G\|+2)(6t+\theta_S+4)^2$, where $\theta_S$ is the length of a shortest identity word and $t$ the first length after which the walk with steps $\varphi(\mathtt a),\varphi(\mathtt b)$ is within relative sup-error $1/2$ of uniform on its coset of $\ker\chi$ ($\chi:H\to\mathbb Z/d$, $d$ the gcd of identity-word lengths; the error is monotone). Proof: value uniqueness of nonterminals, binarization, the mid-subtree counting lemma of (54) applied to all identity words of one length, and independence of prefix/factor/suffix values. **Corollary M1:** a counterexample family to Problem G needs mixing time $t\ge|H|^{1/2-o(1)}$. With the numerics of (198), $H_n$ would get $\|G\|\ge2^n/\mathrm{poly}(n)$ (mixing of $H_n$ not yet proved). Route to a positive answer to Problem G: (R1) directed-walk mixing bounded by the diameter (open), (R2) Breuillard–Tointon, Adv. Math. 289 (2016), Theorem 1.3 (cited), (R3) the determinant argument of (54) Theorem 1 for quotients with a bounded-index abelian subgroup (open). Assessment: the group route to `op:S1` is likely to close; the sprint moves to non-group families for the content typing. Manuscript unchanged.

- **2026-11-07 (190)** **Proposition 7.3 partly answered** (another Claude thread; prompt (188)): `reviews/claude_prop73_2026-11-07.md` and `reviews/check_claude_prop73_2026-11-07.py`. **Theorem 4:** a finite SUB$_1$-closed $Q_3$ lies on a strictly positive weight level $\iff\operatorname{rank}H_3\le2$ (planar Lemmas 2 (wedge, possibly truncated) and 3 (half-strip): a finite closed set in a plane meeting $\Pi_3$ unboundedly is collinear). The prompt's Task-1 conjecture is false: **Example 1** $F_4=\{(1,3,12),(4,8,1),(6,4,4),(12,1,3)\}$ is closed of rank 3 (det 59); the 346 positive-level cases of (187) were a sampling bias. **Theorem 6 (level theorem):** for any reduced linear nondeleting grammar (any fan-out, no substitutability), if all $\mathtt{abc}$-words *shorter than* $\mu$ lie on one level of a strictly positive $\lambda$, then $\mu_{\mathtt{ab}}\le\max\{9(r+1)v\theta,11(r+1)v^2\theta^4\}$. **Lemma 7:** $\mu_{\mathtt{ab}}\le(K_c+3)v\theta$ (improves (178) S3). **Lemma 8 (apex):** in case (ii), $\pi(W)=p_1+p_2-p_{12}$ with three $\mathtt{abc}$-points shorter than $\mu$. **Lemma 9 (domination):** in case (ii) some $\mathtt{abc}$-point has $\mathtt a,\mathtt b$-deficit $\le\theta$. **Corollary 10:** one vertex, SUB$_1$, $Q_3$ finite and rank $\le2$ (or the short part on a positive level) $\Rightarrow$ the bound of Theorem 6; covers $X$ and $Y_{23}$, so no family through $Y_{23}$ with $F_L\to\infty$ can be a counterexample. Rank 3 forces $F_L=\infty$. Families $F_{X,Y}$ (closed by Lemma 9) and $\mathcal S_n$ (rank 3, $K_c=n+1$; closed by Lemma 8 / Theorem 6). **Remaining hole:** Proposition 7.4 (rank 3, short part not on a positive level, $P$ an apex, domination holds) and combinatorial **Conjecture 7.5** ((N1)–(N5′) $\Rightarrow$ short part on a positive level), which would close the one-vertex class. Verification (this thread; Claude wrote and checked, so not independent): proofs read line by line, no mathematical error found (one typo: the $c$-values of $Y_{23}$ in Prop. 11 are $5,1,2,1$); the report's script reproduces §8 exactly (seed 1); own code `reviews/check_claude_verify_prop73_2026-11-07.py` (no report code): closedness, rank/det, (T) directly from Theorem 2.1, the quoted $V$-memberships, apex sets and an own search of (N1)–(N5′) for $F_4$, $F_{X,Y}$ ($4\le X,Y\le15$), $\mathcal S_n$ ($3\le n\le20$), Example 4 (least $\theta=7$), $Y_{23}$, $X$; 1333 finite random closures of rank $\le2$ all on a positive level (verified $\lambda$); cyclic and weight-homogeneous random grammars for Lemmas 5, 7 and Theorem 6 — 0 violations (the Theorem 6 test is weak: few non-vacuous cases). Independent check by ChatGPT pending.

- **2026-11-07 (187)** **Proposition 7.2 answered** (another Claude thread; prompt (186)): `reviews/claude_prop72_2026-11-07.md` and `reviews/check_claude_prop72_2026-11-07.py`. **Theorem A:** one vertex (Parikh-saturated), $L$ in SUB$_1$, $(P^+)$ fails, $Q_3$ infinite $\Rightarrow$ every reduced linear nondeleting presentation has $\mu_{\mathtt{ab}}\le\max\{9(r+1)v\theta,226v^3\theta^3\}$. Proof: **Lemma 1** (grammar side; one vertex, no substitutability): in case (ii) (two incomparable unmarked heavy nodes) $\pi(W)=\pi(W_t)+\pi(W_{t'})-\pi(W_{tt'})\in V\cap Q_2$ with $V:=(q_3+H_3)\cap\{c=0\}$, so $V\cap Q_2=\emptyset$ gives $9(r+1)v\theta$ (Cor. 2(a), also for finite $Q_3$; settles $T_n$ directly); **Theorem S** (language side): $V\cap Q_2\neq\emptyset\Rightarrow F_L=1$, by the sign pattern of the nonnegative differences of $Q_3$: ($\alpha$) a strictly positive one, ($\beta$-I/II) $H_3^0\subseteq H_2$, ($\beta$-III) "columns over a segment" with $H_3^0=\mathbb Zn_0(s,-t,0)$. $F_L$ can be large when $V$ misses $Q_2$ ($E_N$: $F_L=\lceil N/2\rceil$). **Lemma W:** if all words of $L(G)$ containing $\mathtt a,\mathtt b$ lie on one level of $\lambda$ with $\lambda(a),\lambda(b)\ge1$, $\lambda(c)=0$, then $\mu_{\mathtt{ab}}\le7(r+1)v^2\theta^2$ for any ratio (no substitutability; improves the ratio-dependent bound of (155)). **Correction:** the statement in (184)/(186) that settling Proposition 7.2 closes the Parikh-saturated class is wrong — Lemma 3.9's bound $2B$ uses a language quantity not bounded by the grammar; the case $Q_3$ finite, $V\cap Q_2\ne\emptyset$ remains (**Proposition 7.3**), non-vacuous: $Y_{23}=\{(10,23,0),(1,2,5),(1,26,1),(11,10,2),(24,3,1)\}$ is substitutable with $F_L=23$, $\mu_{\mathtt{ab}}=33$. Verified by Claude (this thread): Lemma 1, Cor. 2, F1–F3, Prop. $\alpha$, B1–B3, Prop. $\beta$-I, Prop. $\beta$-III and Theorem S($\beta$-III) (both branches), Lemma W read line by line, Prop. $\beta$-II (5)(6) in outline; script rerun (seed 1, 1 min 23 s, output as in §9); condition (T) checked directly from its definition for $Y_{23}$ (exhaustive, box 60), $F_L(Y_{23})=23$ by enumerating admissible lattices, and $E_2$–$E_5$ on boxes: `reviews/check_claude_verify_prop72_2026-11-07.py`. Independent check by another model not yet requested.

Astra (prompt P6 of (181)) reported a proof with $\mu_{\mathtt{ab}}\le8748(r+1)^3v^3\bar\theta^3$ but hit its usage limit before the report could be retrieved; from the visible fragments Claude reconstructed the outline (P7 of (181)), and another Claude thread wrote the full proof. **Statement:** if $L=L(G)$ has the form (16) $=\{w\in\mathrm{Path}_D(s,t)\cap\Sigma^+:\mathrm{cont}(w)\in\mathcal M,\ \varphi(w)\in g_{\mathrm{cont}(w)}+H\}$ (each letter one edge, $\varphi$ into an abelian group, $H$ shared, cosets per content), then for **every** reduced linear nondeleting MCFG presentation (any fan-out, substitutability not used) $\mu_{\mathtt{ab}}(L)\le\max\{9(r+1)v\theta,\,226v^3\theta^3\}$, $\theta=\max_R\rho(R)\le\bar\theta_G$. Case A ($\{\mathtt{a,b,c}\}\in\mathcal M$, $g_{\mathtt{abc}}-g_{\mathtt{ab}}\notin H$): comparing $W,W_t,W_{t'},W_{tt'}$ shows that no two incomparable unmarked heavy nodes exist; at most three heavy leaves, paths of length $\le3v$. Case B (otherwise): representatives per (label, mark set $M_z$) — shortest yields containing *at least* the letters of $M_z$ — make both witnesses of every $\delta_z$ contain $\mathtt a$ and $\mathtt b$, so $\Phi(\delta_z)\in H$; the telescoping identity holds for any choice of representatives; then an own lattice lemma ($x+y\le2\Theta^2\Theta_0+8\Theta^2+2$) and an Euler path. Verified by Claude (this thread): every proof read line by line (incl. all rank cases of Lemma 5.4 and the constant), script rerun (seed 1, 400 trials, 45 s, output as reported, no violation). Notes: the report's $\theta$ is $\le$ the manuscript's $\bar\theta$, so the theorem holds verbatim with $\bar\theta_G$; case A has only one nontrivial random instance per seed; the report's "reading 2" of $J$ is not Astra's $J$ (Astra's $J$ is case A). **Consequence:** no counterexample to `op:fanout-two` lies in (16). Open: a $v$-free bound, $H$ depending on the content, noncommutative invariants, letters on several edges, the general antichain width $q$. Independent check by another model: prompt (182).

The one-colour heavy nodes that can branch between two consecutive both-letter levels are bounded independently of the both-letter nodes: non-saturated colour $\le18v$, non-frequent $\le v\bar\theta$ (from (112) §3.1–3.2, which only replace one-colour nodes); frequent nodes of ratio $\alpha$ have length in $[\alpha,\bar\theta(1+\alpha)]$ with $\alpha\ge1/\bar\theta$, so below a frequent node of the smaller ratio, and below one of the larger ratio cut at the smaller, there are $\le v(\bar\theta+\bar\theta^2)$ of them. A tree with $H$ branching nodes has $\le(r-1)H+1$ leaves, so each both-letter level multiplies the count by at most $P=[(r-1)c_0+1][(r-1)H_0+1]^2$ ($c_0=18v+v\bar\theta+1$, $H_0=c_0+v(\bar\theta+\bar\theta^2)$), hence $N_{bc}\le\sum_{j\le h}P^j$ and, by Thm 3.2 of (114), $\mu_{\mathtt a}\le\mathrm{poly}_r(v,\bar\theta)^{O(h)}$. If correct: **one both-letter label (or boundedly many) gives a polynomial bound**, and a superpolynomial family needs an unbounded nested chain of distinct heavy both-letter labels (the failed candidate $A_i\to A_{i-1}A_{i-1}\mid D$ has $h=n$). Also asks for the level exchange **(L)**: for nested both-letter nodes $w_1\supset w_2$, the tuples "$w_1\mapsto\rho_{B_1}$" and "all $t_{B_1,B_2}$ copies of $B_2$ under $w_1\mapsto\rho_{B_2}$" share the context of $w_1$; combined with Lemma S, is the $\mathtt a$-count leak forced? Or a family that avoids it.

Mechanism (checked): the $\mathtt a$-ending spine is cut along a balanced binary tree of level intervals into segment classes $(a,b,p)$ whose type does not depend on the inner levels; each class has two members exposing only the first letter at the split point, Linked through one wrap context $\mathtt c^{n-a}\mathtt l\blank C_{b-1}C_{b-1}\mathtt r^{n-a}$ of length $O(n)$; nonterminals of $\mathcal B$ are strings, so classes are not duplicated per parent, and each level lies in $O(\log n)$ sample words. The proof by induction on the tree (Link between the two members, gapless binary Comp, induction) and the length count (disjoint intervals per depth) are correct; the interior part cites Astra round 5 (README (26)). Also correct: the segment method needs $\ge n^2(\log_2n-1)$ for every tree (Kraft/Gibbs), a limit of the method, not of $\mathcal B$; the condition "every level has an $\mathtt l$-node with $\mathtt a$-ending left child" is not necessary under $s_{1,1}$ either; a cut lemma for Comp templates (each side typed-congruent). Recheck by this thread: the report's script reproduces $n=2,3,4$ (all of $F_n$ generated); the independent implementation of $\mathcal B$ from round 2, with its Link key changed from the last letter to the $s_{1,1}$ type, confirms $K_2$ and $K_3$ characteristic and rejects $K^{\mathrm{int}}_3$ and $K^{\mathrm{seg}}_3$ alone. Open: $\Theta(n^2)$ or $\Theta(n^2\log n)$; no family is known that makes Theorem A(i) sharp. Suggested for the manuscript (needs the author): record $n^2+7n+4\le\mathrm{cs}\le O(n^2\log n)$ for $F_n$, $s_{1,1}$ in the paragraph "Tightness of the exponents".

Weak: doubling weights keep $N_\Delta$ linear for polynomial $\Delta$. (b) Lemma 3.1 / Thm 3.2: if $\mathtt a^4$ occurs and all $\mathtt a$-runs have length in $d\mathbb N$ ($d\ge2$), no word has two $\mathtt a$-runs (redistribution); hence the whole schema $E_0\to\mathtt a$, $E_i\to E_{i-1}E_{i-1}\mid d_i$, $d_i\in\{\mathtt b,\mathtt c\}^+$, fails arity 2 for every $n\ge3$ and every choice of $d_i$ (explicit certificate $(\mathtt{aa},\mathtt a)$ vs $(\mathtt a,\mathtt{aa})$, contexts $\blank_1\mathtt a\blank_2\mathtt a^{N-4}$ and $\blank_1u\mathtt a\blank_2u\mathtt a^{N-8}$): a closed route. (c) Thm 4.1: abstract additivity and decodability of merged gaps do not exclude doubling weights (prefix code $\mathtt{bc}^i\mathtt b$ with weights $2^i$), but in the full weight-$N$ token language over $\{\mathtt a\}\cup\{\mathtt{bc}^i\mathtt b\}$, $\mathtt c$-redistribution forces affine weights $t_{i-1}+t_{i+1}=2t_i$ — only for that model, not for general grammars. Not done: $N_\Delta=O(\log(g+\theta))$ for polynomial $\Delta$, and any family in $\Cmcf{2}{c}$ with superpolynomial $\mu_{\mathtt a}$.

Correct: (a) **Theorem** for $W\in\{\mathtt a,\mathtt b\}^+$, $|W|\ge7$: $\{W\}$ is 2-substitutable (trivial typing) iff it is substitutable for every arity iff, up to $\mathtt a\leftrightarrow\mathtt b$ and reversal, $W$ is $x^n$, $x^{n-1}y$, $yx^{n-2}y$ or alternating; necessity from the length-7 classification (118 bad words, 32 orbit certificates checked by the script) and a 7-window transition graph (10 states; Claude rechecked the path argument); sufficiency by direct proofs for every arity (length conservation; end letters; parity of positions for the alternating words) — checked; (b) **Proposition**: for $f\ge2$ and $\mathtt a,\mathtt b$ of one idempotent type $e$, a unique word of type $e$ lying in $\{\mathtt a,\mathtt b\}^+$ has one of these shapes (also for images under an injective morphism); (c) with three letters, every factor of $(\mathtt{abc})^\omega$ and every $p x^N q$ pass, so the shapes are not "unary or binary-periodic" in general; (d) idempotence is needed: for $\mathtt a,\mathtt b\mapsto g$, $g^2=e$, $W=\mathtt{aaabbaba}$ passes. The five shapes do not by themselves give or exclude a lower bound at $f=2$; `op:fanout-two` open.
- **2026-11-02 (70)** ChatGPT report on rare words at fan-out $\ge2$ kept: `reviews/chatgpt_nolift_general_2026-11-02.md` (prompt `reviews/prompt_chatgpt_nolift_general_2026-11-02.md`; not in the manuscript). Results: (a) **abelian groups**: $|H|\le(r+2)\bar\theta_G^2$, hence $\operatorname{ord}\varphi(\mathtt a)\le(r+2)\bar\theta_G^2$, independent of $\|G\|$ (short yields $u_X$, rule defects $z_R$ in the kernel lattice, telescoping over a derivation of $\mathtt a^N$ and a determinant that is a nonzero multiple of the index); (b) the exponent $2$ is optimal ($\mathbb Z/(m^2-1)$, $\mathtt b\mapsto-m$, thickness $\le6m$); (c) **the affine group $H_n=\mathbb Z/(2^n-1)\rtimes\mathbb Z/n$**: every $\varepsilon$-free CFG for the positive word problem has $\|G\|+2\ge2^{2n/3-5}/(4n+1)^2$ (words $W_x=A_xA_{\bar x}$ from blocks $\mathtt{ab},\mathtt{ba}$; a subtree with between $1/3$ and $2/3$ of the leaves determines the value of its interval, and each value fibre has at most $2^{n/3+5}$ words), so $H_n$ satisfies Problem G and is no counterexample; (d) general groups: $|H_{\mathrm{ab}}|\le(r+2)\bar\theta_G^2$, a presentation with relators of length $\le(r+2)\bar\theta_G$, and a grammar with thickness $\le3D$ ($D$ the directed Cayley diameter) and size $O(|H|^2)$. Open: Problem G for all finite groups (the dichotomy 'size or thickness bounds the order'). Novelty unverified (counting technique as in Filmus 2011). Assessment: about 92/100.
- **2026-11-01 (53)** DeepSeek report on Problem G kept: `reviews/deepseek_group_word_problem_2026-11-01.md` (prompt `reviews/prompt_deepseek_group_word_problem_2026-11-01.md`; not in the manuscript). Verified by Claude: all three scripts rerun, outputs reproduced (group checks, exact minima for $n=2,3$, greedy values for $n=4,5,6$, abelian and multiplier tables, 720-case cross-check); the simulation theorem re-derived. Results: (a) exact completeness tests for value-labelled grammars $G_V$ (nonterminals $X_g$, $g\in V\subseteq H$; a shortest unparsable word gives a reachability criterion, no length cut-off); (b) **simulation theorem**: every reduced good rank-$\le r$ CFG $G$ for $\varphi^{-1}(1)\cap\Sigma^+$ yields $V^*$ with $|V^*|\le\|G\|+2$ and $L(G_{V^*})=L$ (prefix values of the flattened rules), so $\|G\|\ge\min|V|-2$; several nonterminals per value give no advantage; (c) for $H_n=\mathbb Z/(2^n-1)\rtimes\mathbb Z/n$: exact $\min|V|=5,13$ ($n=2,3$, $r=2$), greedy upper bounds $37,87,212$ ($n=4,5,6$), about $0.56$–$0.62\,|H_n|$, while $\bar\theta$ stays small ($7$–$50$); so $H_n$ looks like **no counterexample** (size exponential), and a proof of $\min|V|\ge c|H_n|$ would give Problem G positively on $H_n$ ($N\le\|G\|$); (d) for cyclic groups $\min|V|$ is small but $\bar\theta\gtrsim N/3$ in all tests; the multipliers $3,4$ give the same ratio as $2$. Caveat: for $n\ge4$ only greedy upper bounds on $\min|V|$ are known, so the exponential growth of the minimum is an observation. Problem G remains open; a counterexample would need groups with $\min|V|/|H|\to0$ and small thickness. Assessment: about 80/100.
- **2026-11-01 (52)** Literature check for Problem G written: `reviews/literature_problemG_2026-11-01.md` (web search, not exhaustive). No result of the same form found (unverified): descriptional-complexity work (Domaratzki–Pighizzini–Shallit, Pighizzini–Shallit–Wang, the smallest grammar problem, Filmus, Molina Lovett–Shallit) measures grammar size only, and for size alone the question is trivial (doubling gives $O(\log N)$ for $\{(\mathtt a^N)^k\}$, with thickness $\ge N$); the thickness of Yoshinaka is not studied for word problems; Muller–Schupp is structural. Suggested use: state Problem G as an open problem with these references.

Hence a positive answer for $c$ at fan-out one implies **Problem G** (for finite groups, the order of $\varphi(\mathtt a)$ is bounded by a polynomial in the size and thickness of any reduced good CFG for $\varphi^{-1}(1)\cap\Sigma^+$), and a negative answer to Problem G settles `op:S1` negatively and separates fan-outs for $s_{U,2}$ via `cor:two-letter-semilattice`. Candidate: $\mathbb Z/(2^n-1)\rtimes\langle\times2\rangle$ with $\mathtt a\mapsto x+1$, $\mathtt b\mapsto2x$, where $\mathtt a^{2^j}=\mathtt b^{n-j}\mathtt a\mathtt b^j$; whether its word problem has a CFG of polynomial size and thickness is unknown. For infinite targets the finiteness-based constraints (unique pure word, one-coordinate rule, extreme layers, spectra) are void (the prompt omitted that item 6 needs finiteness; corrected). (b) Positive partial result: if no rule has two children with long pure yields of one letter, $t_{\mathrm{typ}}\le3(r+1)|V|\bar\theta_G$ (no substitutability). (c) 'Alignment forcing': yields of one typed nonterminal with one content can be exchanged at any position; Propositions C.1 (doubling with markers) and C.2 (three-level products) give explicit forced words, explaining all failures in large finite searches. Assessment: about 85/100.

(c) A staged-marker family $E_i\to E_{i-1}E_{i-1}\mid\mathtt b\mathtt a^i\mathtt b$ meets all known quantitative necessary conditions but fails at $n=3$; template searches fail at $n=3$ (observation). (d) Positional constraints: two pure run lengths in one context force an arithmetic progression (finite targets: impossible); within one $\mathtt b$-layer no two run vectors differ in exactly one positive coordinate; mixed factors with a common context shift $\mathtt b$-counts by a fixed amount, so cannot occur in the extreme layers. Necessary conditions for a lower-bound family: a superpolynomial pure word together with superpolynomially many distinct counts of the other letter. Assessment: about 70/100.

- **2026-11-01 (36)** ChatGPT report on permutation-closed rare fibres kept: `reviews/chatgpt_parikh_closed_family_2026-11-01.md` (not in the manuscript). Verified by Claude (script reproduced). Claude's Observation A was **wrong**: a common accepting context does not force equal Parikh vectors ($\Sigma^+$ with $x=\mathtt a$, $y=\mathtt{aa}$, $E=\square$); what holds is that every forced word differs from the given one by $\Pi(y)-\Pi(x)$ and keeps the content type; only re-cutting ($E[x]=E[y]$) and power transfer have zero difference. Correct results: a fixed-Parikh layer is substitutable for every typing and fan-out; if the pure fibre lies in one Parikh class, any content-equal pair with nonzero difference sharing an accepting context (e.g. $\mathtt{ca},\mathtt{caa}$) and insertable into a pure context breaks $(1,c_\Sigma)$-substitutability; if all $\mathtt c$-words are in $L$, every pure content fibre is empty or full. So permutation-closed rare fibres do not by themselves give a counterexample; what must be controlled is the set of nonzero differences seen in mixed contexts that leak into pure contexts. Also noted correctly: a counterexample at fan-out two settles the boundary at fan-out two only (membership in $\mathcal C_{2,s}$ does not imply $\mathcal C_{3,s}$); the manuscript already states it per fan-out. Coset languages (Parikh preimages of $v+H$) are substitutable — this is `prop:abelian-coset-filter` of the manuscript. Assessment: about 80/100.
- **2026-11-01 (35)** ChatGPT prompt `reviews/prompt_chatgpt_parikh_closed_family_2026-11-01.md` (not in the manuscript): new design principle for a fan-out-two counterexample under content typings. Observation by Claude, to be verified: every word forced by $(2,s)$-substitutability has the Parikh vector (and, for content typings, the content) of an existing word, so power transfer and re-cutting are special cases; the bracketed and Thue--Morse designs failed because the rare fibre was a single word, whereas a permutation-closed rare fibre (e.g. all words with $2^{n-1}$ letters $\mathtt a$ and $\mathtt b$) absorbs every forced word. Remaining constraints: the two-fibre theorem (the mixed side must take several additive values), exponentially many lengths (report 33), cross-fibre exchanges, and small grammars for the rare fibre. Asked for: the Parikh-preservation proposition, a family meeting (i)--(iii), or a general obstruction showing rare types cannot give an $\mathbf{SL}$ counterexample.

- **2026-11-01 (33)** ChatGPT report on the semilattice reduction kept: `reviews/chatgpt_sl_fanout2_2026-11-01.md` (not in the manuscript). Verified by Claude, all proofs correct (script reproduced): (a) Proposition 1: '$\mathbf{LG}$ is exactly the pseudovariety boundary at fan-out $f$' iff $\mathbf{SL}$ lacks uniform polynomial data at fan-out $f$ ($\langle U_1\rangle=\mathbf{SL}$ via characters $H\hookrightarrow U_1^H$); (b) for $s\preceq t$ and $L\in\mathcal C_{f,s}$, $L(\mathcal B_{f,t}(K))\subseteq L(\mathcal B_{f,s}(K))\subseteq L$ (Link rules of $t$ are Link rules of $s$), so a characteristic sample for $t$ is one for $s$; hence $\mathbf{SL}$ is decided by content typings $c_\Sigma$ alone, for upper and lower bounds; rare-type bounds transfer to content types; (c) a two-hole re-cutting obstruction without powers: for every semilattice typing, a language in $\mathcal C_{2,s}$ containing $P\,t_4\,Q$ ($t_4$ the Thue--Morse word of length 16) contains explicit other words of the same content, one with factor $\mathtt{bbb}$; so a Thue--Morse backbone cannot be the unique word of its type, and cube-free languages cannot contain $t_4$; the natural Thue--Morse doubling family fails at fan-out two for $n\ge4$; (d) for a finite target with $k$ distinct word lengths, every word has length $\le\bar\theta_G+k(k-1)|V|(\|G\|+r\bar\theta_G)$ (any typing, any fan-out), so polynomial data when $k$ is bounded, and an exponential rare-type family needs exponentially many lengths. Open: whether $\mathbf{SL}$ has polynomial data at fan-out two. Assessment: about 90/100.

- **2026-10-31 (19)** Second ChatGPT window-gap report kept: `reviews/chatgpt_window_gap_round2_2026-10-31.md` (not in the manuscript). Verified by Claude, all proofs correct: (a) at fan-out one $L(\mathcal B_{1,s}(K))=L(\mathcal B^{\le2}_{1,s}(K))$ for every $K$ and $s$ (peel off the last child); (b) the syntactic congruence of $F_n$ is equality of partial stack transformations on the reachable stacks; (c) for $u,v\in L_i$ and $b\in\{\mathtt c,\mathtt l\}$, $ub$ and $vb$ are typed-congruent under $s_{0,1}$ (the type sees only the next letter); (d) hence Claude's proposed necessary condition fails: for every $n\ge3$ there is a characteristic sample without any top-level $\mathtt l$-node whose left child ends in $\mathtt a$ (explicit two-word example for $n=3$: $\mathtt{carc}\to\mathtt{laac}$ across a constituent boundary); deleting such words leaves the class; (e) the weight inequality $\sum A_{j-1}\le4|w|$ over all such nodes of a derivation, so the $\Omega(n^3)$ bound holds only under the (false) assumption. The question $\Theta(n^2)$ vs $\Theta(n^3)$ for $F_n$ stays open. Note by Claude: (c) suggests a general mechanism for one-sided windows — segmenting every constituent shifted by $\ell$ letters to the right makes its $s_{0,\ell}$-type a property of the context, so the suffix-type cost disappears except along the right spine, at the price of a prefix condition on each constituent; for $F_n$ the prefix condition is cheap, which points to $O(n^2)$, while a family with rare prefixes and rare suffixes at once is the natural remaining candidate for $\Omega(\|G\|^2\bar\theta_G)$. Assessment: about 88/100.
